Assistant2026-08-31T03:07:16.050Z
Thinking
Assistant2026-08-31T03:07:31.622Z

Here's the whole picture, then where I think it should go.

Two mechanisms, often confused

Accepts — the gate. Declarative, on each plugin: "I need a column of semantic type X." GET /api/plugins?applicable_to=<id> filters by it, which drives the action dropdown and the agent's choices. Matching is hierarchical, so ("categorical",) matches geo.country_iso2. It never ranks — a plugin is applicable or not.

Suggesters — the proposal engine. Five plugins of kind suggester. Each returns Suggestion(title, rationale, kind, score, action) where action is a literal API request body. suggest() runs all five, concatenates, sorts by score. Nothing dedupes and nothing compares across suggesters.

Suggester What it reads Emits Scores
NormalizeSuggester semantic temporal + non-temporal physical type parse this date 0.97
VizSuggester geo.lat/lng, time columns, categorical, measures, rarity types map, timeseries, timeline, bar, histogram 0.95–0.6
AggregateSuggester categorical with 0 < ndv ≤ 500; dimension ≤ 10,000 frequency, rollup, top-K 0.85–0.5
FeatureSuggester time columns + entity columns ≥ 3 distinct behavioural features, feature table 0.88, 0.62
JoinSuggester joinable_with across peer datasets join / annotate 0.7–0.95

Three signals, used inconsistently

Role — by_role("measure"), role == "dimension", time_columns(). What you can do. Meaning — matches_any(semantic_type, ("categorical",)), joinable_with. What it is. Hierarchical everywhere except JoinSuggester, which uses exact equality — so geo.country_iso2 won't match a plain categorical. Name — scattered across five places in two languages.

The whitelists — eleven, in six files

ENTITY_TYPES (what can be an actor) and SUBJECT_TYPES (what a timeline can pivot to) are near-duplicates that differ by one entry, undocumented. RARITY_TYPES + RARITY_COLUMNS are a semantic list plus a legacy name list. ACTOR_WORDS is mine from an hour ago. Six sets of name needles live in detectors. looksLikeColumn lives in the frontend.

And two "boring measure" suffix lists that disagree: _interesting_measures skips _int, _id, _count, _version; _measures in feature_plan skips _id, _seq, _index, _count, _version. Neither is right; both are guesses I wrote at different times.

What's actually broken

1. Scores are uncomparable. Sixteen literal sites, eighteen distinct values, each chosen locally against nothing. The order you see is emergent, no test pins it, and tuning one silently reorders another. When I bumped the timeline to 0.93 I had no way to see what it displaced.

2. The same question has different answers in different files. "Is this a category?" is ndv ≤ 500 in AggregateSuggester and repeats ≥ 3 and uniqueness < 0.9 in feature_plan. I fixed the second one for your browsing log an hour ago and left the first — so agg.frequency still won't be suggested for url. Same bug, same table, one of two places fixed.

3. Name knowledge has no home. It's real and useful — the detectors have leaned on it since the start — but it's six ad-hoc needle lists plus my ACTOR_WORDS plus two suffix lists plus a frontend copy.

Before I propose a shape, two things genuinely need your judgement:

What would you like to clarify?

A few things I could go deeper on, if any of these is what's behind the pause:

  • What "column vocabulary" would actually contain — I gave a five-label sketch (actor / category / measure / clock / identifier), but the real question is whether those labels are the right cut, and whether they're distinct enough from role, which already exists and half-does this. That overlap is a fair thing to be suspicious of.

  • Why role isn't already the answer. Role has six values and is stored per column. The reason suggesters don't just use it: role says "dimension" for both a 5-value country and a 3.4-million-value event id, and that distinction is exactly what every suggester is re-deriving. But you could argue the fix is to make role finer rather than add a second vocabulary.

  • What the intent bands would actually change in practice — I can show you the current top-5 for two or three of your real datasets against what the banded version would produce, so it's a concrete comparison rather than a principle.

  • Scope. These are two fairly large refactors of working code, and "add a ranking test and unify the constants" is a much smaller change that fixes the url bug and the drift without restructuring anything. I may have framed the options as bigger than the problem warrants.

  • Something I've mis-diagnosed. You've been in this UI far more than I have — if the suggestions are wrong in a way I haven't named, that should drive the structure rather than my read of the code.

Tell me which, or what else.

Assistant2026-08-31T03:13:15.828Z
Thinking
Assistant2026-08-31T03:13:32.523Z

"Computed on read" vs "stored"

Stored means a column in the columns table, written during profiling and read back with the profile — exactly how role works today.

Computed on read means a pure function over the profile you already loaded, called whenever a suggester runs. Nothing new persisted.

The argument for computed is sitting in your catalog right now:

logon  date  VARCHAR  time.timestamp  time        774,154
logon  date  VARCHAR  time.date       dimension    10,000

Same column, same file, two versions — one says time, one says dimension. The rule changed two days ago; the old row didn't. A stored derived value records what the rules said at the time it was written. We've already been bitten by that once (time_columns() exists specifically to re-check the physical type because stored roles go stale). Adding a second stored derived field doubles that failure mode.

Cost isn't a reason to store: it's arithmetic over ~20 columns, microseconds. The one thing storing buys is visibility in the schema table, and you can render a computed value just as easily.

Role vs meaning

Three layers, each answering a different question:

Question Set by Example
Type How is it stored? The reader, at import VARCHAR, TIMESTAMP
Meaning What is it, in the world? Detectors, from values net.ip, geo.country_iso2
Role What can I do with it? meaning, vetoed by type dimension, measure, time

role = f(meaning, type) — meaning supplies the intent, type supplies the veto. Four cases from your data show why neither collapses into the other:

Meaning absent, role present. http.user and http.pc: meaning —, role dimension. No detector knows what a CERT username looks like. But they're VARCHAR, so you can group by them. Role has a total fallback; meaning doesn't. Derive role only from meaning and these columns become unusable.

Meaning present, role demoted. logon.date as VARCHAR / time.timestamp / dimension. It means a timestamp — correct — but you can't subtract an interval from text. Two fields let you say "this is a date" (so parsing gets suggested) and "you can't use it yet". Collapse them and you must either lie or throw away the fact.

Same role, different meaning. sender_country (geo.country_iso2) and sender_subject (categorical): both dimensions, both 5 values, operationally interchangeable. But only the country joins to another dataset's country column, and only the country makes a map plausible. Meaning is what crosses dataset boundaries; role never leaves the table.

Same everything, different usefulness. This is the one that matters most:

email_activity  recipient_id   VARCHAR  identity.email  dimension    178   34 events each
email_activity  sender_email   VARCHAR  identity.email  dimension  2,615    2 events each

Identical type, identical meaning, identical role. One is your actor; the other is nearly a per-row identifier. Neither layer can tell them apart — and the same holds for sender_ip, a perfectly good net.ip (5,774 values in 6,000 rows) that is a useless category.

Which answers your suspicion about the vocabulary

I raised the objection myself — "isn't this just role?" — and the honest answer is no, because there's a fourth question with no name:

  • Type: how is it stored?
  • Meaning: what is it?
  • Role: what can I do with it?
  • — : what is it good for here?

The first three are properties of the column's identity. The fourth is a property of its distribution — cardinality, repetition, skew. That's what every suggester re-derives, with a different threshold each time, and it's why ndv ≤ 500 and repeats ≥ 3 are both trying to answer the same unnamed question in different files.

So the proposal isn't a rival vocabulary competing with role. It's naming a layer that already exists implicitly in eleven places.

And that distinction argues for computing it rather than storing it, for a reason beyond staleness: role is pinnable — you can override it in the schema table, and that's right, because "what this means" is a human judgement. But "this column has 2.3 events per value" is a fact about data, not an opinion. It shouldn't be overridable, and anything stored alongside role invites exactly that.

User2026-08-31T03:21:42.477Z

where is the 'role' used? is it used just for suggesters for behavoiral features? create a diagram showing the flow for all of these columns and how each is used for the various plugins. output it as an HTML documentation.

2. Key Technical Concepts

  • Plugin architecture: kind (reader/detector/transform/aggregator/suggester/visualizer) fixes the Python interface; mode (pushdown/batch/external/inspect) fixes execution. Orthogonal. 35 plugins across 6 kinds.
  • Semantic layer: 17 hierarchical types (net.ip, geo.lat, identity.email, numeric.share, numeric.rarity). matches_any is hierarchical; joinable_with is not.
  • Three-way column model: physical type (set by reader, immutable), semantic meaning (detectors, editable/pinnable), role (derived from meaning + storage, editable).
  • QuerySpec IR → DuckDB SQL, deliberately no windows/joins/CTEs. Three sanctioned escape hatches: AggregatePlan.derive, SqlPlan.add, POST /api/query/sql.
  • Feature IR + shorthand: count() by user, activity over 30d, share(), days_since_last(), event_index(), percentile().
  • scrypt password hashing + HMAC-signed session tokens; signing key in data dir.
  • DuckDB specifics: RANGE BETWEEN INTERVAL n DAY PRECEDING, cume_dist(), try_strptime, types={'col':'VARCHAR'}, approx_count_distinct.
  • Storage: ParquetStorage (default) / DuckDBTableStorage; catalog always SQLite WAL.

3. Files and Code Sections

backend/src/dataq/core/features.py (~405 lines) — Feature IR, shorthand parser, to_sql. Recently added percentile:

UNWINDOWED_FNS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(("percentile",))

# in to_sql:
if feature.fn == "percentile":
    part = (f"PARTITION BY {', '.join(quote(k) for k in feature.by)} "
            if feature.by else "")
    # NULL sorts last, so cume_dist would report a missing value as the
    # maximum -- the most extreme thing in the column.
    return (f"CASE WHEN {col} IS NULL THEN NULL ELSE "
            f"cume_dist() OVER ({part}ORDER BY {col}) END")

backend/src/dataq/core/profile.py — is_temporal, ENTITY_TYPES, entity_columns, and the newest actor-ranking logic:

ACTOR_WORDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset((
    "user", "users", "username", "userid", "account", "actor", "owner",
    "employee", "member", "customer", "client", "person", "staff",
    "principal", "recipient", "sender",
))
ACTOR_NAME_BOOST = 1.5

def _named_like_an_actor(name: str) -> bool:
    return any(part in ACTOR_WORDS
               for part in re.split(r"[^a-z0-9]+", name.lower()))

# inside entity_columns.rank():
per_value = stats.row_count / distinct
score = min(distinct, per_value)
if _named_like_an_actor(c.name):
    score *= ACTOR_NAME_BOOST
return -score

4. Errors and Fixes

  • Map drew no points: maplibre-gl-shared.mjs never emitted (?url copies a file without following its imports). Fixed with ?worker&url. Compounded by SpaStaticFiles returning 200 HTML for missing assets — fixed by refusing fallback for paths with a file extension.
  • .dockerignore claim was false: I nearly documented that data/ was excluded; verification showed backend/data (265 MB) shipping in every image. Made patterns recursive (**/data).
  • types={'date':'TIMESTAMP'} aborts whole import; ignore_errors dropped 18 of 43 rows. Chose casting in the materialisation projection instead.
  • Silent aggregate truncation: QuerySpec.limit capped at 1,000,000; feature table needed 3.6M rows, so join left 3 of 5 million rows NULL with job reporting success. Made limit optional and truncation an error.
  • Distinct counts were sampled, so a 38,230-user column looked like a per-row identifier. Moved counts to full-table (0.3s for 11.4M×19).
  • Pinned role copied verbatim, skipping the is_temporal demotion — recreated the VARCHAR - INTERVAL bug. Unified in _storable_role.
  • Timeline threshold control did nothing: abnormality_value honoured only when abnormality_column also supplied; the page sends value alone.
  • Standalone join silently multiplied 6,000 → 31,426 rows. Added allow_fanout guard that drops the result and errors.
  • Grain inference by bucket spacing read five daily buckets 15 days apart as weekly. Switched to date_trunc alignment fingerprint.
  • Actor ranking (twice): first by semantic type (picked category over actor), then by distinct count (picked sender_email 2,615/~2-each over recipient_id 178/~34-each). Settled on min(distinct, rows_per_value) plus a name nudge.
  • Category filter capped at 500 distinct — dropped url (110,491 values × ~31 visits), pc, user, so the http log proposed nothing.
  • Tests read developer's .env: user configured DATAQ_USERS, and 61 tests began returning 401. Fixed with _env_file=None at every Settings( construction in tests.
  • User feedback I acted on: the user chose the two-step aggregate-then-attach architecture for features ("IMO this should be a 2 step process"), which changed the design substantially.
  • My mistake, acknowledged: I ran rm -rf data/lake, destroying the user's imported yellow_tripdata_2016-02. I re-imported all 11,382,049 rows but the dataset id changed and derived datasets were lost.

Delivered: map fix, Railway deploy fix, date-format detection with ambiguity resolution, parse checks, behavioural features (two-step), dataset deletion, text-date role fix, import column plan, username/password auth, feature auto-suggestion, plus two published artifacts.

Tool reply2026-08-31T03:25:48.985Z
        chain: list[str] = []
        cur: str | None = type_id
        seen: set[str] = set()
        while cur is not None and cur not in seen:
            seen.add(cur)
            chain.append(cur)
            st = self._types.get(cur)
            cur = st.parent if st else None
        return chain

    def is_a(self, type_id: str, ancestor: str) -> bool:
        return ancestor in self.ancestry(type_id)

    def matches_any(self, type_id: str | None, accepted: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
        """True if ``type_id`` is, or descends from, any of ``accepted``.

        An empty ``accepted`` means "no constraint".
        """
        if not accepted:
            return True
        if type_id is None:
            return False
        return any(self.is_a(type_id, a) for a in accepted)

    def joinable_with(self, type_id: str | None) -> bool:
        if type_id is None:
            return False
        st = self._types.get(type_id)
        return bool(st and st.joinable)


SEMANTIC_TYPES = SemanticTypeRegistry()


def _r(*args, **kwargs) -> SemanticType:
    return SEMANTIC_TYPES.register(SemanticType(*args, **kwargs))
---------------- base.py applicable ----------------
class Accepts(BaseModel):
    """Preconditions a dataset must satisfy for this plugin to be offered.

    Used by ``GET /api/plugins?applicable_to=`` to answer "what can I do with this
    dataset?" -- which drives both the UI action list and the agent's choices.
    """

    # Needs at least one column of one of these semantic types (descendants count).
    semantic_types: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    dataset_kinds: tuple[DatasetKind, ...] = ()
    min_rows: int = 0

    def matching_columns(self, columns: list[Any]) -> list[str]:
        """Names of columns whose semantic type satisfies ``semantic_types``."""
        if not self.semantic_types:
            return [c.name for c in columns]
        return [
            c.name
            for c in columns
            if SEMANTIC_TYPES.matches_any(getattr(c, "semantic_type", None), self.semantic_types)
        ]


class Produces(BaseModel):
    """What the plugin adds, for lineage display and downstream suggestion."""

    semantic_types: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    dataset_kind: DatasetKind | None = None

base.py
builtin
kinds.py
        self, columns: list[Any], row_count: int, dataset_kind: DatasetKind,
        kind: PluginKind | None = None,
    ) -> list[type[Plugin]]:
        """Plugins whose ``accepts`` preconditions this dataset satisfies."""
        out = []
        for p in self.list(kind=kind):
            a = p.accepts
            if a.dataset_kinds and dataset_kind not in a.dataset_kinds:
                continue
            if row_count < a.min_rows:
                continue
            if a.semantic_types and not a.matching_columns(columns):
                continue
            out.append(p)
        return out

    def _ensure_entry_points(self) -> None:
        """Third-party plugins register via the ``dataq.plugins`` entry-point group,
        so ``pip install dataq-plugin-geoip`` adds capability with no core change."""
        if self._loaded_entry_points:
            return
        self._loaded_entry_points = True
        for ep in entry_points(group=ENTRY_POINT_GROUP):
            loaded = ep.load()
            if isinstance(loaded, type) and issubclass(loaded, Plugin):
                self.register(loaded)

Run the real feature planner on both datasets
cd /Users/dmitrychechik/projects/anthropic/dataq/backend
uv run python - <<'PYEOF'
import sqlite3, json
from dataq.core.profile import ColumnProfile, ColumnStats, DatasetProfile, entity_columns
from dataq.services.feature_plan import propose, _categoricals, _measures

c = sqlite3.connect("file:data/catalog.sqlite?mode=ro", uri=True)
def load(name):
    rows = c.execute("""SELECT col.name, col.physical_type, col.semantic_type, col.role,
      col.stats, v.row_count, v.id FROM columns col
      JOIN dataset_versions v ON v.id=col.version_id JOIN datasets d ON d.id=v.dataset_id
      WHERE d.name=? AND v.version=d.latest_version ORDER BY col.position""", (name,)).fetchall()
    cols=[]
    for n, pt, st, role, stats, rc, _ in rows:
        s = json.loads(stats) if stats else None
        cols.append(ColumnProfile(name=n, physical_type=pt, semantic_type=st, role=role,
                                  stats=ColumnStats(**s) if s else None))
    return DatasetProfile(dataset_id="x", version=1, row_count=rows[0][5], columns=cols)

for ds in ("http", "email_activity"):
    p = load(ds)
    print(f"===== {ds}  ({p.row_count:,} rows) =====")
    print("  entity_columns (actor candidates), ranked:",
          [f"{x.name}({x.stats.distinct_count:,})" for x in entity_columns(p)] or "NONE")
    pr = propose(p)
    print("  actor:", pr.actor, "| time:", pr.time_column)
    if pr.actor:
        print("  _categoricals:", [x.name for x in _categoricals(p, pr.actor)] or "NONE")
    print("  _measures:", [x.name for x in _measures(p)] or "NONE")
    print("  blocked:", pr.blocked or "-")
    for f in pr.features: print("     ", f.expression)
    print()

<div class="wrap">
<header>
  <p class="kicker">DataQ · schema reference · verified 30 Aug 2026</p>
  <h1>Four Layers of a Column</h1>
  <p class="standfirst">Every column in DataQ carries a physical type, a meaning, a role
  and a set of statistics. They are produced at different moments by different code, and
  read by almost entirely disjoint sets of consumers. This traces each one from where it
  is made to everyone who reads it.</p>
</header>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">01</span><h2>Where is role used?</h2></div>

  <div class="verdict">
    <p class="lede">Role has nine consumers, and not one of them executes anything.</p>
    <p>No query planner, no SQL compiler, no job runner and no storage backend reads it.
    Every plugin that needs a date column re-checks the <em>physical</em> type itself
    rather than trusting the role — in seven separate places. Role only ever decides what
    gets <em>offered</em> and how a chart is <em>drawn</em>.</p>
  </div>

  <pre class="evidence">$ grep -rn 'role' core/query.py core/features.py services/jobs.py storage/
<span class="zero">(no matches)</span>

<b>the seven sites that re-check physical type instead of trusting role</b>
  core/profile.py:93          time_columns() filters by_role("time") through is_temporal()
  plugins/builtin/features.py:67    enrich.features
  plugins/builtin/aggregators.py:101 agg.time_rollup
  plugins/builtin/timeline.py:167   viz.timeline
  plugins/builtin/visualizers.py:226 viz.timeseries
  plugins/builtin/suggesters.py:263 suggest.normalize
  services/import_plan.py:200       plan validation</pre>

  <p>So your instinct was close. It is not <em>only</em> the behavioural-feature suggester,
  but every consumer is a suggestion or a presentation decision. Get the role wrong and you
  get a worse menu; get the physical type wrong and <code>enrich.features</code> throws
  <code>VARCHAR&nbsp;-&nbsp;INTERVAL</code> and the job dies.</p>

  <p><strong>Role is advisory. Physical type is enforced.</strong> That asymmetry explains
  most of the surprises in this system, and it is worth holding on to for everything below.</p>
</section>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">02</span><h2>The four layers</h2></div>

  <div class="layers">
    <div class="layer l-type">
      <h3>Physical type</h3>
      <p class="q">How is it stored?</p>
      <dl>
        <dt>made by</dt><dd>reader sniff, then the cast projection</dd>
        <dt>stored</dt><dd><span class="mono">columns.physical_type</span></dd>
        <dt>editable</dt><dd>no — re-import only</dd>
        <dt>enforced</dt><dd><strong>yes</strong>, 7 sites</dd>
      </dl>
    </div>
    <div class="layer l-mean">
      <h3>Meaning</h3>
      <p class="q">What is it, in the world?</p>
      <dl>
        <dt>made by</dt><dd>10 detectors, over values + names</dd>
        <dt>stored</dt><dd><span class="mono">columns.semantic_type</span></dd>
        <dt>editable</dt><dd>yes, pinnable</dd>
        <dt>enforced</dt><dd>only by the <code>Accepts</code> gate</dd>
      </dl>
    </div>
    <div class="layer l-role">
      <h3>Role</h3>
      <p class="q">What can I do with it here?</p>
      <dl>
        <dt>made by</dt><dd>meaning's default, vetoed by type</dd>
        <dt>stored</dt><dd><span class="mono">columns.role</span></dd>
        <dt>editable</dt><dd>yes, pinnable</dd>
        <dt>enforced</dt><dd><strong>never</strong></dd>
      </dl>
    </div>
    <div class="layer l-stat">
      <h3>Statistics</h3>
      <p class="q">What is it good for?</p>
      <dl>
        <dt>made by</dt><dd><span class="mono">compute_stats</span>, full table</dd>
        <dt>stored</dt><dd><span class="mono">columns.stats</span> (JSON)</dd>
        <dt>editable</dt><dd>no — it is a fact</dd>
        <dt>enforced</dt><dd>n/a</dd>
      </dl>
    </div>
  </div>

  <p>The first three describe the column's <em>identity</em>. The fourth describes its
  <em>distribution</em>, and it is the only one with no name in the codebase — which is
  why eleven different places re-derive it with eleven different thresholds.</p>
</section>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">03</span><h2>How each layer is made</h2></div>
</section>
</div>

<div class="wide">
  <div class="flow">
    <div class="lane">
      <span class="lane-label">import</span>
      <span class="node">CSV / Parquet<span class="n-t">source file</span></span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node">reader.to_relation<span class="n-t">DuckDB sniff</span></span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node">cast projection<span class="n-t">try_strptime, from the plan</span></span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node out n-type">physical_type<span class="n-t">frozen for this version</span></span>
    </div>
    <div class="lane">
      <span class="lane-label">profile</span>
      <span class="node">written rows<span class="n-t">the materialised version</span></span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node">compute_stats<span class="n-t">full-table counts</span></span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node out n-stat">stats<span class="n-t">distinct, nulls, top values</span></span>
    </div>
    <div class="lane">
      <span class="lane-label">detect</span>
      <span class="node">10 detectors<span class="n-t">values + column name</span></span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node">confidence &ge; 0.5<span class="n-t">CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD</span></span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node out n-mean">semantic_type<span class="n-t">or None if nothing fires</span></span>
      <span class="arr dim">⇢</span>
      <span class="node">a pin overrides<span class="n-t">human judgement wins</span></span>
    </div>
    <div class="lane">
      <span class="lane-label">derive</span>
      <span class="node out n-mean">semantic_type<span class="n-t">registry default role</span></span>
      <span class="arr">+</span>
      <span class="node out n-type">physical_type</span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="veto">_storable_role() &nbsp;·&nbsp; role "time" on non-temporal storage → "dimension"</span>
      <span class="arr">→</span>
      <span class="node out n-role">role</span>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="wrap">
  <p class="note">The veto in the last lane is the whole reason role and meaning are two
  fields. A text column holding <code>03/07/2011&nbsp;08:07:29</code> <em>means</em>
  <span class="chip t-mean">time.timestamp</span> — correct, and that is what makes
  "parse this" suggestable — but it cannot be a time axis, so its role is
  <span class="chip t-role">dimension</span>. Collapse the two and you must either lie
  about the meaning or lose the ability to offer the fix.</p>
</section>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">04</span><h2>Who reads what</h2></div>
  <p>Every consumer in the backend and frontend, and which layers each one touches.
  The <span class="chip t-name">name</span> column is a fifth input that no model field
  declares — it is read directly off <code>column.name</code>.</p>
  <div class="legend">
    <span><i class="dot d-type"></i> physical type</span>
    <span><i class="dot d-mean"></i> meaning</span>
    <span><i class="dot d-role"></i> role</span>
    <span><i class="dot d-stat"></i> statistics</span>
    <span><i class="dot d-name"></i> column name</span>
  </div>
</section>
</div>

<div class="wide">
  <div class="tw">

      </tr></thead>
      <tbody>

        <tr><td>suggest.aggregate</td><td class="src mono">suggesters.py:182,232</td>
          <td class="ctr no">·</td><td class="ctr"><i class="dot d-mean"></i></td><td class="ctr"><i class="dot d-role"></i></td><td class="ctr"><i class="dot d-stat"></i></td><td class="ctr no">·</td>
          <td>Frequency, rollup, top-K. Own cardinality cutoffs: <code>ndv&nbsp;&le;&nbsp;500</code>, <code>&le;&nbsp;10,000</code>.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>suggest.normalize</td><td class="src mono">suggesters.py:262</td>
          <td class="ctr"><i class="dot d-type"></i></td><td class="ctr"><i class="dot d-mean"></i></td><td class="ctr no">·</td><td class="ctr no">·</td><td class="ctr no">·</td>
          <td>"Parse this date" — precisely the meaning/type mismatch the role veto records.</td></tr>

<div class="wrap">
<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">05</span><h2>Two gates, not one</h2></div>
  <p>The table above splits into two mechanisms that are easy to confuse because both
  answer "what can I do with this dataset".</p>
  <div class="tw"><table>
    <thead><tr><th></th><th>Accepts</th><th>Suggesters</th></tr></thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr><td>Reads</td><td><span class="chip t-mean">meaning</span> only</td>
        <td><span class="chip t-mean">meaning</span> <span class="chip t-role">role</span> <span class="chip t-stat">stats</span> <span class="chip t-name">name</span></td></tr>
      <tr><td>Granularity</td><td>Dataset-level: "at least one column qualifies"</td><td>Column-level</td></tr>
      <tr><td>Matching</td><td>Hierarchical — <code>geo.country_iso2</code> satisfies <code>categorical</code></td><td>Hierarchical, except join, which is exact</td></tr>
      <tr><td>Output</td><td>Boolean — in the menu or not</td><td>A score from 0.5 to 0.97</td></tr>
      <tr><td>Missing meaning</td><td><code>matches_any(None, …)</code> is <strong>always false</strong></td><td>Falls back to role and statistics</td></tr>
    </tbody>
  </table></div>
  <p>That last row is the consequential one. A column whose detectors all declined has
  <em>no</em> meaning, and therefore satisfies <em>no</em> gate — but the gate is
  dataset-level, so the plugin still appears as long as some other column qualifies.
  The menu is then right for the wrong reason.</p>
</section>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">06</span><h2>What that does to your browsing log</h2></div>
  <p>Running the real gate against the real catalog rows for <code>http</code>
  (3,451,665 events):</p>

  <pre class="evidence">columns:  <b>id</b> identity.key · <b>date</b> time.timestamp · <b>user</b> None · <b>pc</b> None · <b>url</b> identity.url

  agg.frequency     needs (categorical, text, boolean)   -> <b>id, url</b>
  agg.topk          needs (categorical, text)            -> <b>id, url</b>
  viz.bar           needs (categorical, text, boolean)   -> <b>id, url</b>
  agg.features      needs (categorical, text, net.ip, …) -> <b>id, url</b>
  normalize.numeric needs (text, numeric)                -> <b>id, url</b></pre>

  <p>Every gate resolves to the same two columns, and both answers are wrong in opposite
  directions. <code>id</code> has one value per row, so a bar chart of it draws 3.45 million
  bars — yet it passes every gate, because meaning knows nothing about distribution.
  <code>user</code> and <code>pc</code>, the two columns you actually want to group by,
  qualify for <strong>nothing</strong>, because no detector recognises a CERT username and
  <code>matches_any(None, …)</code> is false.</p>

  <p>The behavioural-feature draft avoids this because it never consults the gate — it goes
  through <code>entity_columns</code>, which falls back to
  <span class="chip t-role">role&nbsp;=&nbsp;dimension</span> plus statistics. After the
  ranking fix it now produces the right answer:</p>

  <pre class="evidence">actor: <b>user</b>   time: <b>date</b>   categoricals: <b>pc, url</b>
  count() by user, pc over 30d
  days_since_last() by user, pc
  share() by pc
  count() by user, url over 30d
  days_since_last() by user, url
  share() by url</pre>

  <p>Two paths, same dataset, opposite conclusions — one says <code>id, url</code>, the
  other says <code>user, pc, url</code>. Only the second is right, and it is right because
  it is the only one that looks at the distribution.</p>
</section>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">07</span><h2>The undeclared fifth input</h2></div>
  <p>Column names are read in eight places across two languages, with no shared vocabulary
  and no model field:</p>
  <div class="tw"><table>
    <thead><tr><th>Where</th><th class="mono">Needles</th><th>Effect</th></tr></thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr><td>detect.ip</td><td class="mono">ip, addr</td><td rowspan="6">Raise or gate a detector's confidence — so a name can create a <span class="chip t-mean">meaning</span>, which creates a <span class="chip t-role">role</span>.</td></tr>
      <tr><td>detect.country_iso2</td><td class="mono">country, cc, nation</td></tr>
      <tr><td>detect.latlng</td><td class="mono">latitude/lat, longitude/lng/lon</td></tr>
      <tr><td>detect.timestamp</td><td class="mono">time, date, ts, epoch, stamp, _at</td></tr>
      <tr><td>detect.money</td><td class="mono">amount, fare, price, cost, total, revenue</td></tr>
      <tr><td>detect.share</td><td class="mono">rarity · share, freq, frequency, proportion, pct</td></tr>
      <tr><td>entity_columns</td><td class="mono">ACTOR_WORDS &times;19</td><td>Multiplies the actor score by 1.5.</td></tr>
      <tr><td>suggest.viz</td><td class="mono">_int</td><td rowspan="2">Two "boring measure" suffix lists that <strong>disagree with each other</strong>.</td></tr>
      <tr><td>feature_plan._measures</td><td class="mono">_id, _seq, _index, _count, _version</td></tr>
      <tr><td>timeline rarity</td><td class="mono">share, rarity</td><td>Legacy fallback beside the semantic list.</td></tr>
      <tr><td>SchemaForm (frontend)</td><td class="mono">column-ish param names</td><td>Whether a form field becomes a dropdown.</td></tr>
    </tbody>
  </table></div>
  <p>Names are a legitimate signal and the detectors have leaned on them from the start.
  The problem is only that there is no single place to look them up, edit them, or test them.</p>
</section>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">08</span><h2>Two live defects this shape produces</h2></div>

  <div class="alert">
    <h3>Derived columns lose their meaning, so the guards fall through</h3>
    <p>Running the planner on <code>email_activity</code> right now proposes
    <code>percentile(percentile_days_since_same_country)</code> — the percentile of a
    percentile, which is the identity function.</p>
    <p><code>_measures</code> does guard against this, by skipping
    <span class="chip t-mean">numeric.share</span> and
    <span class="chip t-mean">numeric.rarity</span>. But columns written by
    <code>enrich.features</code> carry <em>no</em> meaning at all — nothing re-detects them
    — so the guard never fires, and the suffix list does not contain <code>percentile_</code>.
    Sorting by descending distinct count then floats these derived columns above the real
    <code>urls_in_email</code>.</p>
  </div>

  <div class="alert">
    <h3>The same question, two thresholds, one of them still wrong</h3>
    <p>"Is this column a usable category?" is answered in two places.
    <code>feature_plan</code> asks whether values <em>recur</em>
    (<code>rows/distinct&nbsp;&ge;&nbsp;3</code>); <code>suggest.aggregate</code> asks
    whether there are few of them (<code>ndv&nbsp;&le;&nbsp;500</code>).</p>
    <p>The recurrence test was fixed for your browsing log an hour ago. The cardinality test
    was not — so <code>agg.frequency</code> still will not be suggested for
    <code>url</code>, a column with 110,491 addresses visited 31 times each. Same bug, same
    table, one of two sites repaired.</p>
  </div>
</section>

<section>
  <div class="stage"><span class="num">09</span><h2>Where the seams are</h2></div>
  <p>Three observations fall out of the matrix, each pointing at a different structural
  choice:</p>
  <p><strong>Role earns its place, but not where you would expect.</strong> Its real job is
  the veto — recording that a column's meaning outruns its storage — plus chart encoding.
  As a signal <em>for suggestion</em> it is thin: every suggester that consults it
  immediately reaches for statistics too, because role cannot distinguish a five-value
  country from a 3.4-million-value event id.</p>
  <p><strong>The fourth layer has no name and eleven implementations.</strong> Every
  threshold in this document — <code>ndv&nbsp;&le;&nbsp;500</code>,
  <code>repeats&nbsp;&ge;&nbsp;3</code>, <code>distinct_frac&nbsp;&lt;&nbsp;0.9</code>,
  <code>MIN_ACTOR_DISTINCT</code> — is a private answer to one shared question.</p>
  <p><strong>The gate and the ranker disagree, and the gate is the one that is wrong.</strong>
  <code>Accepts</code> reads the single layer that is most often missing, at the wrong
  granularity, and passes <code>id</code> while rejecting <code>user</code>. Nothing about
  its design is unfixable — it predates both statistics-on-the-full-table and the actor
  ranking — but it is the mechanism a new user meets first.</p>
</section>

<footer>
  <p>Every claim here was checked against the working tree and the live catalog on
  30 August 2026: line numbers from the current sources, the gate simulation and the
  feature drafts by running <code>Accepts.matching_columns</code> and
  <code>feature_plan.propose</code> against the real <code>http</code> and
  <code>email_activity</code> profiles.</p>
</footer>

Assistant2026-08-31T03:30:30.372Z

https://claude.ai/code/artifact/5ac28466-e24a-4431-82fc-294707dc132e — Four Layers of a Column

The direct answer

Role has nine consumers, and none of them execute anything. grep -rn 'role' core/query.py core/features.py services/jobs.py storage/ returns nothing. Every plugin that needs a date column re-checks the physical type itself — in seven separate places — rather than trusting the role.

Your guess was close: not only the behavioural-feature suggester, but every consumer is a suggestion or presentation decision. The one exception is chart.py, where role picks the Vega encoding — the only place it changes output rather than an offer.

Role is advisory, physical type is enforced. Get the role wrong and you get a worse menu; get the physical type wrong and the job dies with VARCHAR - INTERVAL.

What the matrix turned up

Two things I hadn't seen before, both verified by running the real code against your real catalog:

The Accepts gate is wrong for your browsing log, in both directions. Simulating it against http:

agg.frequency / agg.topk / viz.bar / agg.features  ->  id, url

Every gate resolves to the same two columns. id has one value per row — a bar chart of it draws 3.45 million bars — yet it passes everything, because meaning knows nothing about distribution. user and pc qualify for nothing, because no detector recognises a CERT username and matches_any(None, …) is unconditionally false. The gate is dataset-level, so plugins still appear — right menu, wrong reason.

The feature draft gets it right only because it bypasses the gate entirely and goes through entity_columns, which falls back to role plus statistics. Same dataset, two paths, opposite conclusions.

A live bug in the planner. Running propose() on email_activity right now emits percentile(percentile_days_since_same_country) — the percentile of a percentile. _measures does guard against this via numeric.share/numeric.rarity, but columns written by enrich.features carry no meaning at all, so the guard never fires; the suffix list doesn't include percentile_; and sorting by descending distinct count floats them above the real urls_in_email.

And the category threshold I fixed an hour ago was fixed in one of two places — agg.frequency still won't be suggested for url.

What this changes about the design question

The matrix makes the earlier options concrete rather than architectural:

Role earns its place, but its real job is the veto (recording that meaning outran storage) plus chart encoding. As a suggestion signal it's thin — every suggester that reads it immediately reaches for statistics too, because role can't tell a 5-value country from a 3.4-million-value id.

The fourth layer is where the actual decisions live, and it has eleven private implementations. That's now a claim with a table behind it, not a hunch.

I've left both defects unfixed — they're small and mechanical, but they're evidence for whichever direction you pick, and fixing them piecemeal is exactly the pattern that produced them.

$ Bash
Read the semantic type registry
cd /Users/dmitrychechik/projects/anthropic/dataq/backend/src/dataq
echo "################ core/semantic.py ################"
cat -n core/semantic.py
Tool reply2026-08-31T03:34:49.983Z
################ core/semantic.py ################
     1	"""The semantic type registry -- DataQ's metadata layer.
     2	
     3	A semantic type is what a column *means* ("this is an IPv4 address"), as opposed to
     4	how it is stored ("VARCHAR"). Semantic types form a hierarchy, so a rule written for
     5	``categorical`` automatically applies to ``geo.country_iso2``.
     6	
     7	This registry is what makes join suggestion, chart suggestion and normalization
     8	automatic rather than hand-wired: plugins declare which semantic types they accept
     9	and produce, and the suggesters match columns across datasets by type.
    10	"""
    11	
    12	from __future__ import annotations
    13	
    14	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    15	
    16	from .types import ColumnRole
    17	
    18	
    19	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    20	class SemanticType:
    21	    id: str
    22	    title: str
    23	    parent: str | None = None
    24	    # Default role for a column of this type, used when suggesting charts/queries.
    25	    role: ColumnRole = "dimension"
    26	    # Whether matching columns of this type across datasets is a meaningful join.
    27	    # High-cardinality identifiers and shared vocabularies are; free text is not.
    28	    joinable: bool = False
    29	    description: str = ""
    30	    # Physical types this can plausibly sit on top of; used to skip detectors early.
    31	    physical: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    32	
    33	
    34	class SemanticTypeRegistry:
    35	    def __init__(self) -> None:
    36	        self._types: dict[str, SemanticType] = {}
    37	
    38	    def register(self, st: SemanticType) -> SemanticType:
    39	        if st.id in self._types:
    40	            raise ValueError(f"semantic type already registered: {st.id}")
    41	        if st.parent is not None and st.parent not in self._types:
    42	            raise ValueError(f"unknown parent {st.parent!r} for semantic type {st.id!r}")
    43	        self._types[st.id] = st
    44	        return st
    45	
    46	    def get(self, type_id: str) -> SemanticType | None:
    47	        return self._types.get(type_id)
    48	
    49	    def require(self, type_id: str) -> SemanticType:
    50	        st = self._types.get(type_id)
    51	        if st is None:
    52	            raise KeyError(f"unknown semantic type: {type_id}")
    53	        return st
    54	
    55	    def all(self) -> list[SemanticType]:
    56	        return list(self._types.values())
    57	
    58	    def ancestry(self, type_id: str) -> list[str]:
    59	        """``geo.country_iso2`` -> ``['geo.country_iso2', 'categorical', 'text']``."""
    60	        chain: list[str] = []
    61	        cur: str | None = type_id
    62	        seen: set[str] = set()
    63	        while cur is not None and cur not in seen:
    64	            seen.add(cur)
    65	            chain.append(cur)
    66	            st = self._types.get(cur)
    67	            cur = st.parent if st else None
    68	        return chain
    69	
    70	    def is_a(self, type_id: str, ancestor: str) -> bool:
    71	        return ancestor in self.ancestry(type_id)
    72	
    73	    def matches_any(self, type_id: str | None, accepted: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
    74	        """True if ``type_id`` is, or descends from, any of ``accepted``.
    75	
    76	        An empty ``accepted`` means "no constraint".
    77	        """
    78	        if not accepted:
    79	            return True
    80	        if type_id is None:
    81	            return False
    82	        return any(self.is_a(type_id, a) for a in accepted)
    83	
    84	    def joinable_with(self, type_id: str | None) -> bool:
    85	        if type_id is None:
    86	            return False
    87	        st = self._types.get(type_id)
    88	        return bool(st and st.joinable)
    89	
    90	
    91	SEMANTIC_TYPES = SemanticTypeRegistry()
    92	
    93	
    94	def _r(*args, **kwargs) -> SemanticType:
    95	    return SEMANTIC_TYPES.register(SemanticType(*args, **kwargs))
    96	
    97

    99	_r("numeric", "Number", role="measure", physical=("BIGINT", "INTEGER", "DOUBLE", "DECIMAL"))
   100	_r("text", "Text", role="dimension", physical=("VARCHAR",))
   101	_r("temporal", "Date/Time", role="time", physical=("TIMESTAMP", "DATE", "TIME"))

################ catalog/repo.py head + pin ################
"""Catalog repository: the only module that talks to SQLite."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from contextlib import contextmanager
from datetime import UTC, datetime

from sqlalchemy import Engine, inspect
from sqlmodel import Session, SQLModel, create_engine, select

from ..config import Settings
from ..core.profile import ColumnProfile, ColumnStats, DatasetProfile, SemanticGuess
from ..storage.base import StoredRef
from .models import ColumnRow, DashboardRow, DatasetRow, JobRow, StepRow, VersionRow


def make_engine(settings: Settings) -> Engine:
    settings.ensure_dirs()
    engine = create_engine(
        f"sqlite:///{settings.catalog_path}",
        connect_args={"check_same_thread": False},
    )
    SQLModel.metadata.create_all(engine)
    _add_missing_columns(engine)
    with engine.connect() as c:
        # WAL lets the API read while a job worker writes.
        c.exec_driver_sql("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
        c.exec_driver_sql("PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000")
    return engine


def _add_missing_columns(engine: Engine) -> None:
    """Bring an existing catalog up to the current models.

    ``create_all`` creates missing *tables* and silently ignores missing
    *columns*, so adding a field to a model works perfectly against a fresh
    database -- every test -- and breaks the first query against a catalog that
    already exists. Which is to say: it breaks only in deployment, and only
    after the schema change looked fine.

    Additive changes are the only ones handled, because they are the only ones
    that are safe without knowing what the data means. A column that is neither
    nullable nor defaulted cannot be filled in for existing rows, so it is left
    alone and the error it eventually causes says something true.
    """
    inspector = inspect(engine)
    existing_tables = set(inspector.get_table_names())
    with engine.begin() as conn:
        for table in SQLModel.metadata.sorted_tables:
            if table.name not in existing_tables:
                continue
            have = {c["name"] for c in inspector.get_columns(table.name)}
            for column in table.columns:
                if column.name in have:
                    continue
                if not (column.nullable or column.default is not None):
                    continue
                ddl = column.type.compile(engine.dialect)
                conn.exec_driver_sql(
            )

    # --- columns -----------------------------------------------------------
    def set_columns(self, version_id: str, columns: list[ColumnProfile]) -> None:
        with self.session() as s:
            for old in s.exec(select(ColumnRow).where(ColumnRow.version_id == version_id)):
                s.delete(old)
            for i, c in enumerate(columns):
                s.add(
                    ColumnRow(
                        version_id=version_id, position=i, name=c.name,
                        physical_type=c.physical_type, semantic_type=c.semantic_type,
                        confidence=c.confidence, role=c.role, pinned=c.pinned,
                        stats=c.stats.model_dump() if c.stats else {},
                        candidates=[g.model_dump() for g in c.candidates],
                        warning=c.warning,
                    )
                )
            s.commit()

    def pin_column_type(
        self, version_id: str, name: str, semantic_type: str | None, role: str | None = None
    ) -> None:
        """A human edit. Pinning freezes the type against future re-detection."""
        with self.session() as s:
            row = s.exec(
                select(ColumnRow).where(
                    ColumnRow.version_id == version_id, ColumnRow.name == name
                )
            ).first()
            if row is None:
                raise KeyError(f"no column {name!r} in version {version_id}")
            row.semantic_type = semantic_type
            row.confidence = 1.0
            row.pinned = True
            if role:
                row.role = role
            s.add(row)
            s.commit()

    def get_profile(self, dataset_id: str, version: int | None = None) -> DatasetProfile | None:
        v = self.get_version(dataset_id, version)
        if v is None:
            return None
        with self.session() as s:
            rows = list(
                s.exec(
                    select(ColumnRow)
                    .where(ColumnRow.version_id == v.id)
                    .order_by(ColumnRow.position)
                )
            )
        if rows:
            columns = [
                ColumnProfile(
                    name=r.name, physical_type=r.physical_type,
                    semantic_type=r.semantic_type, confidence=r.confidence,
                    role=r.role, pinned=r.pinned,
                    stats=ColumnStats(**r.stats) if r.stats else None,
                    candidates=[SemanticGuess(**g) for g in r.candidates],
                    warning=r.warning,
                )
                for r in rows
            ]
        else:
            # Version exists but has not been profiled yet.
            columns = [
                ColumnProfile(name=c["name"], physical_type=c["physical_type"])
                for c in v.columns_schema
            ]
        return DatasetProfile(
            dataset_id=dataset_id, version=v.version, row_count=v.row_count, columns=columns
        )

    # --- jobs & steps ------------------------------------------------------
    def create_job(self, title: str) -> JobRow:

############ services/context.py ############
     1	"""Application context: the wiring shared by the API, the job runner and the agent.
     2	
     3	Handlers hold no business logic; they resolve a plugin and call into services that
     4	take this context. The agent binds its tools to the same services, not to HTTP.
     5	"""
     6	
     7	from __future__ import annotations
     8	
     9	from dataclasses import dataclass
    10	
    11	from ..catalog.repo import Catalog, make_engine
    12	from ..config import Settings, get_settings
    13	from ..db import Warehouse
    14	from ..query.compiler import QueryCompiler, QueryError, ResolvedSource
    15	from ..storage import StorageBackend, make_storage
    16	from ..storage.base import StoredRef
    17	
    18	
    19	@dataclass
    20	class AppContext:
    21	    settings: Settings
    22	    catalog: Catalog
    23	    warehouse: Warehouse
    24	    storage: StorageBackend
    25	    runner: object | None = None  # ThreadPoolJobRunner; set at startup
    26	
    27	    def resolve_source(self, dataset_id: str, version: int | None = None) -> ResolvedSource:
    28	        """Turn a dataset reference into something usable in a FROM clause."""
    29	        ds = self.catalog.get_dataset(dataset_id)
    30	        if ds is None:
    31	            raise QueryError(f"unknown dataset: {dataset_id}")
    32	
    33	        row = self.catalog.get_version(dataset_id, version)
    34	        if row is not None:
    35	            stored = StoredRef(**row.stored_ref)
    36	            columns = {c["name"]: c["physical_type"] for c in row.columns_schema}
    37	            return ResolvedSource(sql=self.storage.sql_source(stored), columns=columns)
    38	
    39	        # Unmaterialised join/aggregate datasets are backed by a view.
    40	        if ds.view_sql:
    41	            with self.warehouse.cur() as conn:
    42	                rel = conn.sql(ds.view_sql)
    43	                columns = dict(zip(rel.columns, [str(t) for t in rel.types], strict=True))
    44	            return ResolvedSource(sql=f"({ds.view_sql})", columns=columns)
    45	
    46	        raise QueryError(f"dataset {dataset_id} has no materialised version")
    47	
    48	    def compiler(self) -> QueryCompiler:
    49	        return QueryCompiler(self.resolve_source)
    50	
    51	
    52	def build_context(settings: Settings | None = None) -> AppContext:
    53	    settings = settings or get_settings()
    54	    settings.ensure_dirs()
    55	    catalog = Catalog(make_engine(settings))
    56	    return AppContext(
    57	        settings=settings,
    58	        catalog=catalog,
    59	        warehouse=Warehouse(settings),
    60	        storage=make_storage(settings),
    61	    )

############ import_plan.py: models + validate ############
TARGET_TYPES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "VARCHAR", "BIGINT", "DOUBLE", "BOOLEAN", "DATE", "TIMESTAMP")

# Rows read to build the proposal. Enough to answer the question rather than
# punt it: at 200 sampled values a MM/DD/YYYY column reads as ambiguous, because
# every day in the first 200 rows of a real export can be <= 12. At 1,000 it
# resolves, in 89ms. The whole proposal costs ~70ms on a 5-column file and
# ~200ms on a 19-column one.
PLAN_SAMPLE_ROWS = 2_000

# Preview rows returned alongside the proposal.
PREVIEW_ROWS = 8

# Rows scanned to settle a format the sample could not. A prefix sample is a
# poor witness for day-first versus month-first: the first 2,000 rows of a
# time-sorted export span a single day, so every day in them is <= 12 and both
# readings fit. Asking the file how often each reading actually fails settles
# it -- 109ms over 200,000 rows of the reported file, which found day-first
# failing 114,076 times. Bounded rather than whole-file so the cost does not
# grow with the data.
SETTLE_ROWS = 200_000


class PlanError(ValueError):
    """The plan cannot be carried out as written."""


class ColumnPlan(BaseModel):
    """What to do with one column. The editable part of a proposal."""

    name: str
    # None means "leave it as the reader produced it".
    target_type: TargetType | None = None
    # How to read the text, when the target is temporal and the source is not.
    # A strptime format, or epoch:s / epoch:ms / epoch:us.
    format: str | None = None
    semantic_type: str | None = None
    role: ColumnRole | None = None
    # Set when a person changed this from what was proposed. Only those are
    # frozen against re-detection: accepting a proposal is not an override, and
    # marking every column as one would make "pinned" meaningless.
    pinned: bool = False


class ColumnProposal(BaseModel):
    """A proposed plan for one column, with the evidence behind it."""

    name: str
    source_type: str = Field(description="What the reader would produce, untouched")
    proposed: ColumnPlan
    sample_values: list[Any] = []
    # Why this is proposed, in a sentence, for the person deciding.
    rationale: str = ""
    # Temporal candidates, best first. Offered as choices when more than one.
    formats: list[FormatCandidate] = []
    # Share of sampled non-null values the proposed cast would keep, when it
    # casts at all. Reported rather than enforced: a stray 'n/a' should cost one
    # value, not the import.
    parse_rate: float | None = None
    # True when the data genuinely cannot settle the question and a person must.
    decision_required: bool = False
    conflict: str | None = None


class ImportPlan(BaseModel):
    reader: str
    sampled_rows: int
    columns: list[ColumnProposal]
    rows: list[list[Any]] = []

    @property
    def undecided(self) -> list[str]:
        return [c.name for c in self.columns if c.decision_required]


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# validation
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def validate_plan(plans: list[ColumnPlan], known: set[str]) -> None:
    """Reject a plan that cannot mean what it says.

    ``pin_column_type`` validates neither semantic types nor roles, so a plan
    arriving over HTTP is the first place either is checked.
    """
    seen: set[str] = set()
    for plan in plans:
        if plan.name not in known:
            raise PlanError(
                f"no column named {plan.name!r}; the file has: "
                f"{', '.join(sorted(known)[:12])}"
            )
        if plan.name in seen:
            raise PlanError(f"two plans for the same column: {plan.name!r}")
        seen.add(plan.name)

        if plan.semantic_type and SEMANTIC_TYPES.get(plan.semantic_type) is None:
            raise PlanError(
                f"{plan.name}: unknown semantic type {plan.semantic_type!r}")
        if plan.role and plan.role == "time":
            # profile_columns copies a pinned role verbatim, so a pinned
            # role="time" on a text column recreates the VARCHAR - INTERVAL
            # failure this whole feature exists to prevent. Refused at the door.
            target = plan.target_type or ""
            if not is_temporal(target):
                as_what = target or "whatever the reader produces"
                raise PlanError(
                    f"{plan.name}: role 'time' needs a date or timestamp column, "
                    f"but it is being imported as {as_what}. Set the type to "
                    "TIMESTAMP, or pick another role."
                )
        if plan.format and plan.target_type not in ("TIMESTAMP", "DATE"):
            raise PlanError(
                f"{plan.name}: a format only applies when importing as a "
                f"TIMESTAMP or DATE, not {plan.target_type or 'the reader default'}"
            )

############ JoinSuggester ############
class JoinSuggester(Suggester):
    """Propose joins to other datasets, matched by semantic type.

    This is the payoff of the semantic layer: two datasets are joinable when they
    share a *meaning*, not merely a column name.
    """

    id: ClassVar[str] = "suggest.join"
    title: ClassVar[str] = "Suggested joins"
    Params: ClassVar[type[BaseModel]] = NoParams

    def suggest(self, ctx: SuggestCtx) -> list[Suggestion]:
        p = ctx.profile
        out: list[Suggestion] = []
        joinable: list[ColumnProfile] = [
            c for c in p.columns if SEMANTIC_TYPES.joinable_with(c.semantic_type)
        ]
        for peer in ctx.peers:
            if peer.dataset_id == p.dataset_id:
                continue
            for left in joinable:
                for right in peer.columns:
                    if right.semantic_type != left.semantic_type:
                        continue
                    score = 0.9 if left.name == right.name else 0.7
                    # A join onto a small aggregate is an annotation, which is the
                    # most useful kind, so rank it up.
                    annotation = peer.row_count and peer.row_count < max(1, p.row_count) / 10
                    if annotation:
                        score = min(0.95, score + 0.15)
                    out.append(Suggestion(
                        title=(f"Annotate with {peer.dataset_id[:8]} on {left.name}"
                               if annotation
                               else f"Join {left.name} to {right.name}"),
                        rationale=f"both columns are {left.semantic_type}",
                        kind="join", score=score,
                        action={
                            "op": "join", "plugin_id": "",
                            "inputs": [{"dataset_id": p.dataset_id},
                                       {"dataset_id": peer.dataset_id}],
                            "params": {"left_column": left.name,
                                       "right_column": right.name, "how": "left"},
                        },
                    ))
        return sorted(out, key=lambda s: s.score, reverse=True)[:20]
############ profiler.profile_columns branches ############

def profile_columns(
    stats: list[ColumnStats], previous: list[ColumnProfile] | None = None
) -> list[ColumnProfile]:
    """Resolve each column to a semantic type, preserving human-pinned edits."""
    pinned = {p.name: p for p in (previous or []) if p.pinned}
    out: list[ColumnProfile] = []
    for st in stats:
        guesses = run_detectors(st)
        prof = ColumnProfile(
            name=st.name, physical_type=st.physical_type, stats=st, candidates=guesses
        )
        if st.name in pinned:
            # A human said what this column is; never overwrite that.
            prev = pinned[st.name]
            prof.semantic_type = prev.semantic_type
            prof.confidence = 1.0
            prof.role = _storable_role(prev.role, st.physical_type)
            prof.pinned = True
        elif guesses and guesses[0].confidence >= CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD:
            best = guesses[0]
            prof.semantic_type = best.semantic_type
            prof.confidence = best.confidence
            st_def = SEMANTIC_TYPES.get(best.semantic_type)
            prof.role = _storable_role(
                st_def.role if st_def else "dimension", st.physical_type)
        else:
            prof.role = _fallback_role(st)
        out.append(prof)
    return out


def _storable_role(role: str, physical_type: str) -> str:
    """A role the column can actually fulfil as stored.

    A column's *meaning* can be temporal while its storage is not. Role answers
    "what can I do with this", so a text date is a dimension until it is parsed
    -- otherwise every time-based plugin picks it up and fails on
    VARCHAR - INTERVAL deep inside DuckDB. The semantic type is kept, which is
    what makes the fix suggestable.

    Applied to pinned roles too. A pin is a person's judgement about meaning,
    and deserves to win; it is not a licence to subtract an interval from a
    string.
    """
    if role == "time" and not is_temporal(physical_type):
        return "dimension"
    return role


def _fallback_role(st: ColumnStats) -> str:
    pt = st.physical_type.upper()
    if pt.startswith(("TIMESTAMP", "DATE", "TIME")):
        return "time"
    if pt.startswith(("BIGINT", "INTEGER", "DOUBLE", "FLOAT", "DECIMAL", "HUGEINT",
                      "SMALLINT", "TINYINT", "REAL")):
        return "measure"
    return "dimension"

    18	/**
    19	 * Importing a dataset, with the column types shown before they are frozen.
    20	 *
    21	 * A column's physical type is decided by the reader the moment the file is
    22	 * read, is copied into every record of the dataset, and can never be changed
    23	 * afterwards — the only escape is a transform that adds a second column beside
    24	 * it. Meaning and role, by contrast, are worked out later and stay editable
    25	 * forever. So the one decision that cannot be revisited was the one nobody was
    26	 * ever shown, which is how a column of dates ends up stored as text.
    27	 *
    28	 * The plan arrives already decided. The normal path is to look at it and press
    29	 * Import; the only thing that stops is a column the data genuinely cannot
    30	 * settle, and then it says which readings are on offer and what each would mean.

   181	export function ImportPanel() {
   182	  const navigate = useNavigate()
   183	  const operation = useOperation()
   184	  const [uri, setUri] = useState('')
   185	  const [name, setName] = useState('')
   186	  const [plan, setPlan] = useState<ImportPlan | null>(null)
   187	  const [edits, setEdits] = useState<Record<string, ColumnPlan>>({})
   188	  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null)
   189	  const [jobId, setJobId] = useState<string | null>(null)
   190	  const [browsing, setBrowsing] = useState(false)
   191	  const [planning, setPlanning] = useState(false)
   192	
   193	  const { data: semanticTypeRows } = useQuery({
   194	    queryKey: ['semantic-types'],
   195	    queryFn: api.semanticTypes,
   196	    staleTime: Infinity,
   197	  })
   198	  const semanticTypes = semanticTypeRows?.map((t) => t.id) ?? []
   199	
   200	  async function buildPlan(target = uri) {
   201	    setError(null)
   202	    setPlan(null)
   203	    setJobId(null)
   204	    setPlanning(true)
   205	    try {
   206	      const next = await api.planImport(target)
   207	      setPlan(next)
   208	      setEdits(
   209	        Object.fromEntries(
   210	          next.columns.map((c) => [
   211	            c.name,
   212	            // An undecided column starts blank, so Import stays disabled until
   213	            // somebody actually chooses rather than accepting a coin toss.
   214	            c.decision_required ? { ...c.proposed, format: null } : c.proposed,
   215	          ]),
   216	        ),
   217	      )
   218	    } catch (e) {
   219	      setError(e instanceof ApiError ? e.message : String(e))
   220	    } finally {
   221	      setPlanning(false)
   222	    }
   223	  }
   224	
   225	  /** Picking a file fills the path, names the dataset after it, and plans it. */
   226	  function handlePicked(picked: string) {
   227	    setUri(picked)
   228	    if (!name) {
   229	      const base = picked.split('/').pop() ?? ''
   230	      setName(base.replace(/\.(gz)$/, '').replace(/\.[^.]+$/, ''))
   231	    }
   232	    buildPlan(picked)
   233	  }
   234	
   235	  async function goToNewDataset(job: Job) {
   236	    try {
   237	      const full = await api.job(job.id)
   238	      const dataset = full.steps?.[0]?.outputs?.[0]?.dataset_id
   239	      if (dataset) navigate(`/datasets/${dataset}`)
   240	    } catch {
   241	      // Landing on the list is a fine outcome; it refreshes either way.
   242	    }
   243	  }
   244	
   245	  const undecided = (plan?.columns ?? []).filter(
   246	    (c) => c.decision_required && !edits[c.name]?.format,
   247	  )
   248	
   249	  async function doImport() {
   250	    setError(null)
   251	    try {
   252	      const accepted = await operation.mutateAsync({
   253	        op: 'import',
   254	        uri,
   255	        name: name || undefined,
   256	        params: plan ? { columns: Object.values(edits) } : undefined,
   257	      })
   258	      setJobId(accepted.job_id)
   259	    } catch (e) {
   260	      setError(e instanceof ApiError ? e.message : String(e))
   261	    }
   262	  }
   263	
   264	  return (
   265	    <section className="rounded-lg border border-slate-200 bg-white p-4">
   266	      <h2 className="mb-3 text-base font-semibold text-slate-900">Import a dataset</h2>
   267	
   268	      <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-2">
   269	        <div className="flex min-w-96 flex-1 rounded border border-slate-300 focus-within:ring-1 focus-within:ring-slate-400">
   270	          <input
   271	            className="flex-1 rounded-l px-3 py-1.5 text-sm outline-none"
   272	            placeholder="Choose a file, or type a path or glob"
   273	            value={uri}
   274	            onChange={(e) => {
   275	              setUri(e.target.value)
   276	              // A plan for the previous file would be a lie about this one.
   277	              setPlan(null)
   278	            }}
   279	          />
   280	          <button
   281	            type="button"
   282	            onClick={() => setBrowsing(true)}
   283	            className="rounded-r border-l border-slate-300 bg-slate-50 px-3 text-sm text-slate-700 hover:bg-slate-100"
   284	          >
   285	            Browse…
   286	          </button>
   287	        </div>
   288	        <input

   290	          placeholder="name (optional)"
   291	          value={name}
   292	          onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)}
   293	        />
   294	        <button

   296	          onClick={() => buildPlan()}
   297	          disabled={!uri || planning}

   299	        >
   300	          {planning ? 'Reading…' : plan ? 'Re-read' : 'Read columns'}
   301	        </button>

   303

   308	          <p className="mb-1 text-xs text-slate-500">
   309	            Read by <code className="font-mono">{plan.reader}</code> ·{' '}
   310	            {plan.columns.length} columns · types worked out from{' '}

p = "core/semantic.py"
s = open(p).read()

# --- 1. module docstring gains the custom-type paragraph -------------------
old_doc = '''This registry is what makes join suggestion, chart suggestion and normalization
automatic rather than hand-wired: plugins declare which semantic types they accept
and produce, and the suggesters match columns across datasets by type.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from .types import ColumnRole
'''
new_doc = '''This registry is what makes join suggestion, chart suggestion and normalization
automatic rather than hand-wired: plugins declare which semantic types they accept
and produce, and the suggesters match columns across datasets by type.

The built-in types below are the ones DataQ can *detect*. They are not meant to be
the whole vocabulary: no detector will ever recognise a fleet's machine names or an
internal cost-centre code, and a dataset full of columns nothing recognises is a
dataset nothing can suggest anything about. So the registry also holds *custom*
types, defined by a person and persisted in the catalog -- see
``services.semantic_types``. Saying that ``pc`` here and ``device`` there both mean
``machine.name`` is what makes the two joinable, and it is a claim only a person can
make.

Custom types are ordinary members of the hierarchy: they name a parent, inherit
matching from it, and are indistinguishable to every consumer. Only two things
separate them -- they can be edited and deleted, and they are never produced by a
detector.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace

from .types import ColumnRole

# A custom type's id. Dotted segments are a naming convention rather than a
# structure -- ``geo.lat`` has parent ``numeric``, not ``geo`` -- but they read
# well and keep a vocabulary sorted into families.
TYPE_ID_RE = re.compile(r"^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]*)*$")
MAX_TYPE_ID_LENGTH = 64
'''
assert s.count(old_doc) == 1
s = s.replace(old_doc, new_doc)

# --- 2. registry gains the custom-type methods ----------------------------
old_reg = '''class SemanticTypeRegistry:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._types: dict[str, SemanticType] = {}

    def register(self, st: SemanticType) -> SemanticType:
        if st.id in self._types:
            raise ValueError(f"semantic type already registered: {st.id}")
        if st.parent is not None and st.parent not in self._types:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown parent {st.parent!r} for semantic type {st.id!r}")
        self._types[st.id] = st
        return st
'''
new_reg = '''class SemanticTypeError(ValueError):
    """A custom type cannot mean what it says."""


class SemanticTypeRegistry:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._types: dict[str, SemanticType] = {}
        self._builtins: frozenset[str] = frozenset()

    def register(self, st: SemanticType) -> SemanticType:
        if st.id in self._types:
            raise ValueError(f"semantic type already registered: {st.id}")
        if st.parent is not None and st.parent not in self._types:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown parent {st.parent!r} for semantic type {st.id!r}")
        self._types[st.id] = st
        return st

    # --- built-in vs custom ------------------------------------------------
    def seal_builtins(self) -> None:
        """Everything registered so far is built in; anything later is custom.

        Called once at the bottom of this module. The distinction matters in
        exactly two places: a person may edit or delete their own types but not
        the ones plugins are written against, and only custom types need loading
        from the catalog at start-up.
        """
        self._builtins = frozenset(self._types)

    def is_builtin(self, type_id: str) -> bool:
        return type_id in self._builtins

    def custom(self) -> list[SemanticType]:
        return [t for t in self._types.values() if t.id not in self._builtins]

    def add_custom(self, st: SemanticType) -> SemanticType:
        """Register a person-defined type, replacing one of the same id.

        Replacement rather than refusal because this is an edit surface: a type
        defined with the wrong parent should be correctable without first being
        deleted out from under the columns using it.
        """
        self.validate_custom(st)
        self._types[st.id] = st
        return st

    def validate_custom(self, st: SemanticType) -> None:
        if st.id in self._builtins:
            raise SemanticTypeError(
                f"{st.id!r} is a built-in type and cannot be redefined")
        if len(st.id) > MAX_TYPE_ID_LENGTH:
            raise SemanticTypeError(
                f"{st.id!r} is too long; {MAX_TYPE_ID_LENGTH} characters at most")
        if not TYPE_ID_RE.match(st.id):
            raise SemanticTypeError(
                f"{st.id!r} is not a usable id. Use lowercase words separated by "
                "dots or underscores, starting with a letter -- for example "
                "'machine.name' or 'cost_centre'."
            )
        if st.parent is not None:
            if st.parent not in self._types:
                raise SemanticTypeError(
                    f"unknown parent {st.parent!r} for {st.id!r}; "
                    f"known types: {', '.join(sorted(self._types)[:12])}"
                )
            # Walking up from the parent must terminate somewhere other than
            # here, or matching this type would depend on itself.
            if st.id in self.ancestry(st.parent):
                raise SemanticTypeError(
                    f"{st.parent!r} already descends from {st.id!r}; "
                    "a type cannot be its own ancestor"
                )

    def reset_custom(self) -> None:
        """Forget every custom type. The registry is a process-wide singleton,
        so this is how a new catalog -- a different data directory, or the next
        test -- avoids inheriting the last one's vocabulary."""
        for type_id in list(self._types):
            if type_id not in self._builtins:
                del self._types[type_id]
'''
assert s.count(old_reg) == 1
s = s.replace(old_reg, new_reg)

# --- 3. seal at the bottom -------------------------------------------------
s = s.rstrip("\\n") + '''


# Everything above is built in and immutable; everything registered from here on
# is somebody's own vocabulary, loaded from the catalog.
SEMANTIC_TYPES.seal_builtins()
'''
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("core/semantic.py updated")

    def list_semantic_types(self) -> list[SemanticTypeRow]:
        with self.session() as s:
            return list(s.exec(select(SemanticTypeRow).order_by(SemanticTypeRow.id)))

    def upsert_semantic_type(self, row: SemanticTypeRow) -> SemanticTypeRow:
        """Define a meaning, or edit one that exists.

        Upsert rather than insert because the id *is* the identity: two people
        deciding independently that a column means ``machine.name`` should land
        on the same type, not on a conflict.
        """
        with self.session() as s:
            existing = s.get(SemanticTypeRow, row.id)
            if existing is not None:
                existing.title = row.title
                existing.parent = row.parent
                existing.role = row.role
                existing.joinable = row.joinable
                existing.description = row.description
                s.add(existing)
                s.commit()
                s.refresh(existing)
                return existing
            s.add(row)
            s.commit()
            s.refresh(row)
            return row

    def delete_semantic_type(self, type_id: str) -> bool:
        with self.session() as s:
            row = s.get(SemanticTypeRow, type_id)
            if row is None:
                return False
            s.delete(row)
            s.commit()
            return True

    def columns_using_type(self, type_id: str) -> list[tuple[str, str, str]]:
        """``(dataset_id, dataset_name, column)`` for every column of this type.

        Across all versions, not just the latest: a type still spelled out in an
        older version's metadata is still in use, and deleting it would leave
        that version describing itself in a vocabulary nothing can read.
        """
        with self.session() as s:
            rows = s.exec(
                select(DatasetRow.id, DatasetRow.name, ColumnRow.name)
                .join(VersionRow, VersionRow.dataset_id == DatasetRow.id)
                .join(ColumnRow, ColumnRow.version_id == VersionRow.id)
                .where(ColumnRow.semantic_type == type_id)
            ).all()
        seen: dict[tuple[str, str], tuple[str, str, str]] = {}
        for dataset_id, dataset_name, column in rows:
            seen.setdefault((dataset_id, column), (dataset_id, dataset_name, column))
        return sorted(seen.values(), key=lambda r: (r[1], r[2]))

'''
